# KayEdu — Build Order (Phase 1)

> Turns "huge project" into an ordered sequence of buildable milestones. Each milestone is a set of Claude Code sessions with a clear **done** definition. Build the **single-center daily loop first** (that's the value a center pays for); portability and portals follow. Don't jump ahead — later milestones assume earlier ones are solid.

**Legend:** each milestone lists **Goal**, **Build**, **Done when** (its acceptance gate).

**Gate policy (DEPLOY-1; CLAUDE.md working rule 8).** The "Gates" and "Done when" lines below name the gates a milestone must satisfy; they are not a per-change checklist. After an ordinary change run the gates for the touched area plus `tsc` and `eslint`. The full `npm run check:all` and `npm run e2e` run only when asked, or before a deployment — and then only against a staging database. Gates that write data, and `e2e`, never run against the shared dev project as routine verification.

---

## M0 — Environment & skeleton
**Goal:** the app runs, connects to Supabase, migrates, and renders a themed shell in AR/EN.
**Build:** scaffold Next.js; add deps; drop in `schema.prisma`, `docs/`, `CLAUDE.md`; `.env`; `prisma migrate dev --name init`; next-intl `[locale]` routing + `ar.json`/`en.json`; base layout with RTL + a placeholder brand color; shadcn init + a few primitives; Prisma singleton (`lib/db/client.ts`).
**Done when:** `npm run dev` shows a themed layout that flips AR(RTL)/EN(LTR) via the locale switch, and the DB has all tables.

## M1 — Identity, auth & tenancy (the backbone)
**Goal:** real users, memberships, center selection, and enforced isolation primitives.
**Build:** Supabase Auth (email/password) + `User` mirror on first login; `Membership`, `Center`, `CenterSettings`; `/select-center` hub; `getTenantContext()`; `lib/db/scoped.ts`; `lib/auth/permissions.ts`; route-group role gates; staff **invite** flow (`Invitation` + accept). Seed one center, one owner.
**Done when:** you log in, land on select-center (or auto-select), enter the center dashboard scoped to it, and a hardcoded test proves a user of Center A cannot load a Center B record by ID.

## M2 — Center configuration / master data
**Goal:** a center can set up its academic + operational reference data.
**Build:** Settings screens for Education Systems (name + base price), Grades (name + system), Subjects (name + grade), Rooms (name + capacity, per branch), Branches, Teacher-Timeout, Card Customization (JSON), default center-cut. Users & Roles (invite/add). All CRUD scoped + audited where sensitive.
**Done when:** a fresh center can be fully configured through the UI with no SQL, and adding a *second* center and configuring it needs zero code changes.
> **Superseded in part.** Education Systems / Grades / Subjects stop being centre-authored at **M13** (a centre picks from the global catalog instead), and the **Branches** screen and per-branch room grouping go away at **M11** when the `Branch` model is retired. This entry stays as the historical record of what M2 built; `00-master-product-spec.md` §4.1 item 10 describes the current shape.

## M3 — People: teachers & students
**Goal:** manage the humans.
**Build:** Teachers (add/edit/deactivate/delete; detail page; teacher block-list). Students (add/edit/delete; `StudentProfile` with parent info + `studentCode`; enrollment view; grade promotion; print ID card + paper). Duplicate-match on register/add. Supabase Storage for avatars + generated ID cards (with QR).
**Done when:** you can register a student once and connect them to the center; a printable QR ID card generates; a teacher can be blocked-list a student; duplicate phone/email triggers match not duplicate.

## M4 — Classes, schedules & session generation
**Goal:** the academic structure and its calendar.
**Build:** ClassGroup CRUD (subject/grade/system/room/price/**center-cut config**/capacity/attendance flags/late threshold/accepts-new/requires-confirmation); ClassSchedule (multi-day); Enrollment (add/remove/block); the **session generator** (rolling window + holiday calendar); teacher/room **conflict** detection.
**Done when:** a class with two weekly slots generates correct future sessions, a room/teacher double-book is prevented/warned, and enrolling past capacity is blocked.

## M5 — The daily loop ⭐ (reception → attendance → payment → receipt)
**Goal:** the beating heart — what a center opens every day. **This is the milestone that sells.**
**Build:** Today's Sessions dashboard (filters). Session detail: enrolled roster + add student (existing/new, drop-in or enroll) + mark attendance (Present/Late/Absent/Excused). **Reception Desk**: search by code/name/phone (+ QR scan), select session, method, status. Payment recording that **freezes the snapshots** via `lib/money.ts` in a transaction. Session receipt (total, center share, teacher share, present/absent, consecutive-absence counts). Void/correct with audit.
**Done when:** all money acceptance tests pass — fixed *and* percentage cuts compute correctly; a recorded payment is unchanged after the class cut changes; a drop-in records attendance+payment with no enrollment; consecutive-absence shows on the receipt.
**Delivered:** all of the above, gated by `npm run check:payments`. QR "scan" is a hardware barcode-scanner-friendly text match on Reception's search box (`lib/id-card.ts`'s `parseStudentQrPayload`), not a camera — no camera code exists in this app. **Not built in M5** (closed at **M10**, tracked below rather than here): session cancel/reschedule/edit (docs/02 §11); consecutive-absence surfaced on the student profile page (session receipt only for now); a per-class "Excused" toggle (all four statuses are offered on every class).

## M6 — Analytics, expenses & the Center Dashboard
**Goal:** owners see the money and the trends.
**Build:** Analytics with date filters + by-teacher (total revenue, center share, teacher share, expenses, net, averages; students total/new, teachers total/active/new) — **all read from frozen snapshots**. Expenses (categories + entries + filters). Receptionist is denied profit views. **The Center Dashboard also lands here** (spec §4.1 item 1) — it was never assigned to an earlier milestone (M1 shipped only a placeholder with people-count stat cards) and needs exactly the revenue/attendance aggregation queries this milestone builds, so `/dashboard` and `/analytics` both call `lib/db/analytics.ts` with different date-range defaults (today vs. day/week/month/year/all) instead of duplicating the math.
**Done when:** analytics numbers reconcile exactly with the underlying payment snapshots for any date range, and a receptionist cannot reach profit by UI or URL. **Delivered:** the above, gated by `npm run check:analytics` — reconciliation across an in-range/out-of-range date split, by-teacher `groupBy` correctness, and cross-center isolation on every analytics/expense query, plus a direct assertion that RECEPTIONIST holds neither `analytics.center.view` nor `expense.manage` (grantable or not). **Not built in M6** (tracked as a fast-follow, not silently dropped): the Approvals screen referenced by spec §4.1 item 9 doesn't exist yet — **built in the Approvals milestone**, below — so at M6 the Dashboard's "outstanding operational items" still surfaces only today's not-yet-started sessions.

## M7 — Teacher portal (cross-center)
**Goal:** the portability payoff for teachers.
**Build:** teacher dashboard with **center switcher**; combined + per-center schedule; sessions (search/filter, detail, print, edit description within deadline); students (search, per-teacher history); analytics per visibility; request new class/session (`ChangeRequest`) → center **Approvals** screen; block student.
**Done when:** a teacher shared by two centers sees a correct combined schedule and earnings, each scoped to that center's granted visibility, with **zero** cross-center leakage (the flagship acceptance test).
**Delivered:** teacher dashboard (`/teacher/dashboard`) — combined KPI row (centers, sessions today/this week, earnings this month) plus one card per center, each computed by its own centerId-scoped query (`lib/db/teacher-portal.ts`'s `teacherCenterSummary`) and only unioned in memory (`combineTeacherSummaries`), never a query spanning centers; the "center switcher" is the existing `/select-center` cookie flow, reused as-is. Sessions (`/teacher/sessions`, `/teacher/sessions/[id]`) — search by student, filter by date/status/grade, roster/attendance as a read-only view (marking attendance/payment stays a center-staff action), absent count with the consecutive-absence alert, earning + center earning (always shown here — this is the teacher's own session, a different thing from the center-staff `center.profit.view` gate), print (`/teacher/sessions/[id]/print`), and an unconditional description edit (`updateDescription`, pinned to `centerId` **and** `teacherId`). Students (`/teacher/students`, `/teacher/students/[id]`) — search by code/name/phone narrowed to students this teacher has an attendance record with (`teacherStudentProfileIds`), detail shows only sessions attended **with this teacher** (`studentSessionsWithTeacher`), and reuses the `StudentBlock` scope TEACHER mechanism from M3 with `refId` always resolved server-side from `ctx.user.id` — never a client-sent teacher id. Classes (`/teacher/classes`) — a request form (subject, grade, timing, capacity, attendance settings) writes a `ChangeRequest` (type `NEW_CLASS`) and lists the teacher's own past requests. Upload files for sessions is a "coming soon" placeholder card on the session detail page, per spec. Gated by `npm run check:teacher-portal` (`scripts/teacher-portal-check.ts`) — the flagship shared-teacher, two-center, zero-leakage scenario, plus a regression check that a shared teacher's block at one center does not carry to the other.
**Not built in M7** (closed at **M10** except where noted, tracked below rather than here): the **Approvals** screen that would review a `ChangeRequest` — still missing, as flagged at M6, and **built in the Approvals milestone**, below; this milestone only added the write path (`scoped(ctx).changeRequests`) and a teacher's own "my requests" list, so at M7 a request sat `PENDING` with no reviewer yet. A dedicated, filterable-by-grade **Analytics** screen (spec §4.2 item 1: "total earnings, total sessions, unique students attended") — the Dashboard's combined/per-center summary covers the same ground at a coarser grain; `/teacher/analytics` stays a disabled nav placeholder, **still not built at M10** (a deliberate scope call, see M10 below). A **teacher edit-deadline** setting for session descriptions — closed at M10. A **"trusted teacher"** flag — closed at M10. **Request new session** (`ChangeRequest` type `NEW_SESSION`, spec §4.2 item 2) — closed at M10.

## M8 — Student portal & discovery/reservation (fast-follow)
**Goal:** students self-serve; the network layer switches on.
**Build:** student schedule (past/future, detail); **Discover** (browse published offerings with filters); reserve/enroll with teacher validation (auto-accept vs confirm) → `Reservation`; my reservations; capacity/duplicate handling.
**Done when:** a student reserves a class, the center/teacher confirms per rule, and the class appears in the student's personal schedule across connected centers.
**Delivered:** `/student/schedule` (upcoming from active enrollments + past from `SessionAttendee` history, unioned per center via `getStudentContexts`, read-only, filterable by date); `/student/discover` (marketplace grid across every active center, docs/04 §10, subject/grade/day filter pills, subject-accent top border, status badge, teacher avatar+local-name, price via `lib/format.ts`, respecting `acceptsNewStudents` and both `StudentBlock` scopes); `/student/reservations` (a student's own PENDING/CONFIRMED/REJECTED/CANCELLED requests across every center, with a cancel action on their own PENDING ones). The write path (`lib/db/reservations.ts`'s `reserveOrEnroll`) branches on `ClassGroup.requiresConfirmation` exactly as specified — instant enroll writes an ACTIVE `Enrollment` directly with no `Reservation` row at all; confirmation-required writes a PENDING `Reservation`, reusing the same row across a reject/re-request cycle rather than erroring on the `(classGroupId, studentId)` unique constraint. A brand-new sign-up with zero relations anywhere can still reach Discover and make their first reservation/enrollment — `requireStudentUser()` (`lib/auth/tenant.ts`) is a looser gate than `requireExperience`, requiring only a signed-in `User`; `/select-center`'s empty state now links to Discover as the way out of what was previously a dead end for a self-registering student (docs/02 §4). Gated by `npm run check:student-portal` (`scripts/student-portal-check.ts`) — instant-enroll vs. requires-confirmation branching, capacity enforcement (checked at request time for instant-enroll, deferred to confirm time for requires-confirmation), duplicate-reservation/duplicate-enrollment prevention, both `StudentBlock` scopes, and the shared-teacher-style cross-center isolation scenario.
**Not built in M8** (tracked as a fast-follow, not silently dropped): the **Approvals** screen a confirmation-required reservation needs — still missing at M8, flagged at M6, M7, and now here a third time, and **built as its own small milestone right after this one**, below. `confirmReservation`/`rejectReservation` (`lib/db/reservations.ts`) are real, transaction-safe, capacity/block-rechecking functions with full gate coverage, but at M8 nothing in the product UI called them yet. Discover's subject/grade filters match on center-local master-data *names*, not ids, and are applied to the full combined result set in the application layer rather than pushed into each center's database query (docs/02 §14) — fine at Phase 1's pilot scale, a deliberate simplification to revisit if the platform grows past a handful of centers.

## Approvals (landed as its own small milestone between M8 and M9)
**Goal:** close the reviewer-UI gap flagged at M6, M7 and M8 — a `ChangeRequest`/`Reservation` sitting `PENDING` forever with nobody able to accept or decline it. Never assigned to a milestone number of its own in this document until now; small enough that it doesn't warrant renumbering M9/M10.
**Build:** `/approvals` (OWNER/MANAGER only, `approvals.manage`) — a combined queue of every PENDING `ChangeRequest` (type `NEW_CLASS`) and every PENDING `Reservation` at the active center. Accept/decline for both entity types, each audited.
**Done when:** accept/decline on both entity types changes status correctly, is audited, and is denied to a role without the right permission; Center A staff cannot see or act on Center B's pending requests.
**Delivered:** the queue described above, plus the decision record spec §4.1 item 9 now carries: accepting a `ChangeRequest` **flips its status only** — `lib/db/approvals.ts`'s `resolveChangeRequest`, a plain PENDING→APPROVED/DECLINED transition that refuses to re-resolve an already-resolved row — it does **not** auto-create a `ClassGroup`, because the request payload has no price/center-cut/room for staff to have decided yet (CLAUDE.md rule 2: never guess at money). Staff create the real class manually at `/classes`, reading the approved request's subject/grade/day/time/capacity/attendance details off the Approvals card. Accepting a `Reservation`, by contrast, immediately calls M8's `confirmReservation` (creates the `StudentProfile` if needed + an ACTIVE `Enrollment`) since a reservation already has everything an enrollment needs. Every accept/decline writes an `AuditLog` row (`changeRequest.approve`/`changeRequest.decline`/`reservation.confirm`/`reservation.reject`) — the first gate script in this codebase to assert on `AuditLog` shape at all. Gated by `npm run check:approvals` (`scripts/approvals-check.ts`) — both entity types' accept/decline, the no-ClassGroup-side-effect assertion, audit-row shape, RECEPTIONIST denial (mirroring M6's `analytics.center.view`/`expense.manage` pattern), and cross-center isolation on the pending queue and on each accept/decline call.
**Not built** (tracked as fast-follows, not silently dropped): **contact** and **edit-and-confirm** (spec §4.1 item 9's fuller "accept/decline/contact/edit-and-confirm") — only accept/decline exist; a manager cannot message a requester or adjust a request's details before confirming it from this screen. **Punted at M10 too, deliberately**: "contact" would need a messaging/thread entity that doesn't exist anywhere in this app — notifications are explicitly deferred (spec §2), and building one just for this one screen is new infrastructure beyond a hardening milestone's scope. "Edit-and-confirm" has no obvious shape for either entity as the data model stands today: a `Reservation` doesn't carry fields worth editing before confirming (the student's choice of class is exactly what confirming acts on), and a `NEW_CLASS` `ChangeRequest`'s missing fields (price/cut/room) are precisely the ones CLAUDE.md rule 2 forbids guessing at — "edit" would mean designing a new class-creation flow embedded in this screen, not a small addition. Revisit if a real center asks for either. `ChangeRequest` type `NEW_SESSION` **is now built** (M10) — see M10 below; the queue shows both `NEW_CLASS` and `NEW_SESSION` requests, in separate sections. No smart "prefill a new class from this approved request" convenience on `/classes` — staff re-type what they already see on the Approvals card.

## M9 — Super Admin (platform)
**Goal:** operate the platform safely.
**Build:** create/activate/suspend center tenants; plan/onboarding metadata; controlled privileged support access (audited); platform KPIs (active centers/teachers/students, sessions, adoption); global reference data where appropriate.
**Done when:** a new center tenant can be provisioned end-to-end from the admin panel, and privileged access to tenant data is logged.
**Delivered (lifecycle/plan reworked at M19):** `/admin/centers` — `createCenterAction` provisions a Center + its CenterSettings row + a founding-Owner `Invitation` in one nested write (`lib/db/platform.ts`'s `createCenterWithSettings`), mirroring how `configureCenter` provisions a fresh center's config at M2 (no per-center branching); the invite link is shared manually, reusing `/settings/staff`'s exact mechanism, because a brand-new center otherwise has no member who could ever reach that screen to invite a first owner. A freshly provisioned center starts **ONBOARDING**, not ACTIVE (M19). The list shows every tenant with staff/teacher/student counts (`platformCenterList`, one `groupBy` per role-bearing relation rather than fetch-and-count) and its lifecycle-state badge. `/admin/centers/[id]` (M19) offers a **lifecycle-transition control** (`transitionCenterLifecycleAction`, only the states `validNextLifecycleStates` allows from the centre's current one) and a **data-visibility-window override** (`updateCenterVisibilityWindowAction`) in place of the original activate/suspend toggle and `Center.plan` editor, plus per-center stats, and writes an `AuditLog` row (`platform.support.access`) on every visit — the "controlled privileged support access" the spec asks for, deliberately just an audited read-only view rather than a full masquerade/impersonation system. `/admin/platform` — operational center count (ONBOARDING/ACTIVE/GRACE) with a suspended-or-archived hint, total teachers, total students, total sessions, and an adoption trend (centers created per month, last six months, read as a plain table rather than a chart), every figure through an aggregate/`groupBy`/`count` in `lib/db/platform.ts`, the same "never fetch-and-sum" discipline M6 established. **Global reference data** — nothing concrete needed it yet at Phase 1 scale, so nothing was built for it, per the milestone's own "where appropriate." Gated by `npm run check:admin` (`scripts/admin-check.ts`) — center creation/lifecycle-transition (valid and invalid)/visibility-window-write through the real production functions, platform KPIs reconciled as a delta (they are deliberately not `centerId`-scoped — a platform admin spans every tenant by design), per-center list/detail stats reconciled exactly, and every support-access view asserted to write exactly one audit row with the expected shape.

## M10 — Hardening, QA & pilot-readiness
**Goal:** trustworthy enough to run a real center.
**Build:** run **all 15 acceptance scenarios** as automated + manual tests; performance pass on rush attendance; audit coverage; backups + tested restore; error monitoring; data import (onboarding) + export (offboarding); privacy review for minors' data; seed a realistic demo center for meetings.
**Done when:** every acceptance criterion in `00-master-product-spec.md` §7 passes, and one real center can run a day of live sessions in a preview environment.
**Delivered:**

*Acceptance criteria (spec §7), automated vs. manual.* Cross-referencing all 15 against the nine milestone gates that already existed found genuine gaps at only the two the task brief predicted — **14 (performance)** and **15 (RTL)** — plus one narrower gap inside an existing gate: **8 (attendance correction is audited)** was checked for permission denial (`check:config`) but never for the actual `AuditLog` write, since that write lives inline in the Server Action rather than in a `lib/db/*` function a gate could import. Closed by reproducing the exact write `setAttendanceAction` performs (`payments-check.ts`, the same pattern `approvals-check.ts` already used for its own audit assertions) and asserting its shape/scoping. New gates: `npm run check:performance` (`scripts/rush-performance-check.ts`) — not a browser benchmark (CLAUDE.md rule 1), it validates the actual risk docs/02 §14 flags: asserts `connection_limit` is set, times a serial vs. concurrent batch of real `createAttendance`+`recordPayment` calls, and asserts the concurrent batch beats naive serial extrapolation (proof the pool grants real concurrency) while every write still lands correctly under the rush (no lost/duplicate rows, revenue reconciles exactly). `npm run check:rtl` (`scripts/rtl-check.ts`) — static-only, for the same reason: en/ar key parity across all ~1,090 translation keys, a scan for physical-direction Tailwind utilities anywhere under `app/`/`components/` (found zero), a scan for inline `toLocaleString` calls outside `lib/format.ts` (found zero), and confirmation the root layout wires `dir`/`lang` from the locale. The other 13 criteria were already covered: 1/2 by `check:teacher-portal`/`check:student-portal`'s shared-teacher/shared-student no-leak scenarios, 3/4/5/13 by `check:payments`, 6/7 by `check:classes`, 9 by `check:analytics`'s permission-map assertion, 10 by `check:isolation`, 12 by `check:people`'s duplicate-match coverage — 11 (cancelled session) was the one real gap in this group, closed below.

*Flagged gaps, resolved.* **Session cancel/reschedule** (spec §7 criterion 11, the docs/02 §11 note this build order carried since M5): `lib/db/sessions.ts`'s `cancelSession`/`rescheduleSession`, a `session.cancel`/`session.manage`-gated UI on the session detail page. Cancelling never touches attendance/payment rows (CLAUDE.md rule 2) — a payment that needs undoing stays `voidPayment`'s separate, explicit, audited job. Rescheduling re-runs the generator's own `findSessionConflicts` (WARN/DENY per the center's `scheduleConflictPolicy`) and refuses a same-class duplicate slot. **Consecutive-absence on the student profile page** (M5's other flagged gap): the student detail page now shows a per-class "absent N times in a row" alert for every active enrollment with a live streak, using the same `consecutiveAbsenceCount` the session receipt already called. **Per-class Excused toggle**: `ClassGroup.allowExcused` (default `true`, matching the pre-M10 "always offered" behavior), a settings checkbox, and a server-side refusal (`excusedNotAllowed`) everywhere EXCUSED could be submitted — the roster's `AttendanceControl` also hides the button when the class disallows it. **Teacher edit-deadline**: `CenterSettings.teacherEditDeadlineHours` (`null` = no deadline, the M7 default), a General Settings toggle mirroring the teacher-timeout pattern, enforced in `updateSessionDescriptionAction`. **Trusted-teacher flag**: `Membership.isTrustedTeacher` (default `true`), a toggle on the teacher's profile form, gating both request forms (`changeRequest.create` is necessary but not sufficient — see docs/02 §6). **`ChangeRequest` type `NEW_SESSION`**: a teacher requests a one-off session on a class they already teach (`/teacher/sessions`'s new request card); unlike `NEW_CLASS`, the request's `ClassGroup` already has a decided price/cut, so `lib/db/approvals.ts`'s `resolveSessionRequest` creates the `Session` immediately on approval — no money to guess at, the `confirmReservation`-style "approve == act" shape, not the "approve == status flip" one. **Punted, deliberately** (see the Approvals section above for the reasoning): **contact** and **edit-and-confirm** on the Approvals screen — no messaging entity exists anywhere in this app, and neither entity type has an obvious "what would you edit" shape without inventing a new flow.

*Pre-deployment checklist (spec §2.1), worked as far as reasonable pre-pilot.* **B (data & tenant security):** real RLS policies and a `rls_auto_enable()` lockdown are drafted in `supabase/m10-rls-and-storage.sql` — deliberately **not** auto-applied by any migration command, since this is security-sensitive SQL against a live Supabase project; it needs a human review-and-run. The same file provisions the `logos/`/`session-uploads/` buckets the checklist still listed as missing. **C (operations & pipeline):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` wires `prisma migrate deploy` and all twelve `check:*` gates (plus typecheck/lint/`prisma validate`) into CI — it needs `DATABASE_URL`/`DIRECT_URL` repo secrets pointed at a dedicated CI/staging project before it can actually run; that secret provisioning is a GitHub-side action outside this session's reach. `db:seed` now populates the seeded center with realistic reference data (an education system, 3 grades, 5 subjects, 3 rooms), 4 teachers + a manager + a receptionist, 20 students, 5 classes with schedules, a full rolling session window (via the real `generateSessions`), and 4 weeks of attendance/payment history (via the real `createAttendance`/`recordPayment` — never fabricated numbers) plus a couple of expenses — idempotent, and off with `SEED_DEMO_DATA=false` for the old bare-owner seed. Left open, needing either a product decision or access this session doesn't have: **A** (email confirmation, leaked-password protection and a transactional email sender are Supabase-dashboard/vendor decisions, not code — `SEED_OWNER_PASSWORD` rotation and `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` are per-environment operational steps), the rest of **B** (service-role key hygiene is already correct in code — never `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` — but is an operational discipline to maintain, not a one-time fix; backups + a tested restore need a live project to test against), the rest of **C** (error monitoring, the cron registration for session generation, and full data import — CSV *export* would have been in-scope-sized but wasn't reached this pass; punting an untested half-feature seemed worse than punting the whole thing cleanly), and **D**'s nav-placeholder sweep (`/teacher/analytics` is the one still-disabled item, a deliberate M7 scope call, not an oversight).

*Performance pass on rush attendance entry.* `.env.example`'s `connection_limit=10&pool_timeout=20` (docs/02 §14) already matched the documented guidance; `check:performance` is what turns "should work" into "does work, measured on this database" — see above.

---

# Phase 1, part two — M11–M21

> M0–M10 built and hardened the single-centre daily loop. M11–M21 build the decisions settled in `00-master-product-spec.md` §5.1: the organization/branch layer, a real permissions engine, the global academic catalog, money completeness (refunds and settlements), the enrolment and approval rules, parents, ratings, a UX/data-integrity sweep, the visibility window and lifecycle states, then deployment — and, only after the pilot, offline.
>
> **Ordering is not arbitrary.** M11 comes first because every later milestone's scope depends on whether "a centre" means one branch or several. M12 comes second because the permissions engine is what M13–M19 hang their new actions on, and retrofitting a matrix onto nine milestones of hardcoded checks costs far more than building on it. Nothing after M12 should be adding a hardcoded role check.
>
> **Every milestone touching money, permissions or isolation names its `check:*` gate.** That is the same non-negotiable M5/M6 established, extended: a milestone in this range without a gate on its money/permission/isolation surface is not done, whatever the UI looks like.

## M11 — Organization / branch restructure
**Goal:** an organization is the paying customer and a centre is one branch under it, **without changing how isolation works**.
**Build:** the `Organization` model (global, no `centerId`); `Center.organizationId`; **retire the `Branch` model** — drop `Room.branchId`/`Session.branchId`, rooms and sessions belong to the centre directly. Owner memberships resolve **org-level** (every branch); Manager/Receptionist/Accountant resolve to exactly one branch; **teacher memberships unchanged**. Navbar **branch switcher** ("Whole Management" vs. one branch), reusing the existing `/select-center` cookie flow rather than adding a second switching mechanism. **Org-level analytics roll-up** (one branch or all) as a union of per-branch `centerId`-scoped queries summed in memory. The **i18n naming layer**: UI says "Branch" for `Center`, "Center" for `Organization` (`02-architecture.md` §8) — message keys only, **no code renames**. No branch-count limit.
**Done when:** an organization with two branches shows a correct all-branches roll-up that equals **exactly** the sum of the two per-branch figures; a user scoped to Branch A cannot read a Branch B record by URL or API; and `lib/db/scoped.ts`, `getTenantContext()`'s scoping and `npm run check:isolation` are **byte-for-byte unchanged** in their isolation behaviour.
**Gates:** `npm run check:branches` (new — roll-up reconciliation + branch-A-can't-read-branch-B), and **`npm run check:isolation` must still pass untouched** — that is the load-bearing assertion of this milestone, not a formality.
**Delivered:** the migration (`prisma/migrations/20260906220000_m11_organization_branch_restructure`) backfills one `Organization` per pre-existing `Center` (reusing the Center's own id as the Organization's id — a real 1:1, not a fresh seed) before making `organizationId` `NOT NULL`, then drops `Branch`/`Room.branchId`/`Session.branchId`. **Owner org-level resolution is a read-time widening in `getUserCenterRelations()` (`lib/auth/tenant.ts`), not a schema change to `Membership`** — the alternative the milestone brief offered (a nullable `Membership.centerId` with real org-level Owner rows) was passed over specifically to keep `lib/db/scoped.ts` and every non-Owner code path untouched; see `02-architecture.md` §4 for the full reasoning and its one known gap (an Owner doesn't yet appear in a sibling branch's own staff list — closes with M12's `MembershipBranchGrant`). The branch switcher (`/select-center`) gained a "Whole Management" card, gated to organizations with more than one branch — it submits the same `selectCenterAction` with that organization's id, and the **existing** `kayedu_center` cookie holds it exactly as it would a branch id (no second cookie: an early version of this milestone added one, corrected during review). `getTenantContext()` is the single place that value is interpreted — branch id first, then an organization the user is actually an Owner of — and it always resolves one real `centerId` for every operational screen, exposing the org id only via `TenantContext.rollupOrganizationId` for Analytics to read. `/analytics` sums per-branch `lib/db/analytics.ts` calls via the new `lib/db/organizations.ts` and renders a per-branch breakdown table as visible proof of the reconciliation; the by-teacher table is hidden in roll-up mode rather than showing an incomplete active-branch-only list. `lib/db/platform.ts`'s `createCenterWithSettings` (Super Admin provisioning) now creates a fresh single-branch `Organization` alongside every new `Center`. The old Branches settings screen and its CRUD (`branchSchema`, `db.branches`) are removed. The i18n naming-layer sweep is complete across the whole app (message values only, both locales) — not just the screens this milestone touched. `npm run check:isolation`'s own fixture swapped from the retired `Branch` model to `Room` (a like-for-like non-money model) since the old fixture no longer exists; its assertions are unchanged. Nine pre-existing gate scripts needed a one-line `organization: { create: { name } }` addition to their throwaway-center fixtures, now that `Center.organizationId` is required — a compatibility fix, not an assertion change, and every one of those gates (`check:config`, `check:people`, `check:classes`, `check:payments`, `check:analytics`, `check:teacher-portal`, `check:student-portal`, `check:approvals`, `check:performance`) still passes.

## M12 — Permissions engine, ACCOUNTANT, and two security fixes
**Goal:** replace the hardcoded role map and the two boolean grants with a configurable action-level matrix, and close two real leaks while the permission seam is open.
**Build:** the `PermissionGrant` matrix (`centerId × role × action × allowed`) covering every role and effectively every action; **`ACCOUNTANT`** added to the `Role` enum with a baseline of analytics + expenses, configurable thereafter; the `/settings/permissions` matrix screen (**OWNER only**); **cross-branch grants** (`MembershipBranchGrant`) so an Owner may widen a branch-scoped role's reach; removal of `receptionistCanViewProfit`/`receptionistCanCorrectAttendance` and `permissionGrantsFromSettings()`; removal of the unread `Membership.canSeeEarnings`. **Security fix 16** — a centre Owner/Manager viewing a teacher must not see that teacher's other centres. **Security fix 17** — a role or grant change invalidates or re-resolves the session immediately.
**Done when:** deny-by-default holds (an action with no row is denied to everyone, including a newly added one); the **grantable ceiling** holds in code (a TEACHER cannot be granted `center.profit.view` however the matrix is written, an OWNER cannot be stripped of ownership actions); a MANAGER cannot change the matrix, assign a role, or grant a branch — by UI **or** by direct action call; a demoted user's very next request resolves the new role; and a teacher's other memberships are invisible to a centre by UI and by API.
**Gates:** `npm run check:permissions` (new — matrix resolution, deny-by-default, OWNER-only granting, the ceiling, session invalidation, the teacher-detail leak). Every existing gate that asserts a denial (`check:analytics`, `check:approvals`, `check:teacher-portal`) is re-pointed at the matrix and must stay green — if a role silently gains something in the migration, those are what catch it.
**Delivered:** `PermissionGrant` (`centerId × role × action × allowed`) and `MembershipBranchGrant` (`membershipId × centerId`, one row per extra branch) land in two migrations — `20260906223000_m12_accountant_role` adds `ACCOUNTANT` to the `Role` enum on its own (Postgres refuses to use a new enum value in the same transaction that added it), then `20260906224000_m12_permission_matrix` creates both tables, **backfills** `PermissionGrant` rows reproducing the pre-M12 hardcoded map plus whatever each center's two settings booleans had actually granted (OWNER/MANAGER get the old `MANAGER_PERMISSIONS` baseline, RECEPTIONIST its five-action baseline plus `center.profit.view`/`attendance.correct` only where that center had switched them on, TEACHER its three-action baseline, and ACCOUNTANT — new, so no history to preserve — gets `analytics.center.view` + `expense.manage` fresh), and only then drops `CenterSettings.receptionistCanViewProfit`/`receptionistCanCorrectAttendance` and `Membership.canSeeEarnings`. `lib/db/platform.ts`'s `createCenterWithSettings` (Super Admin provisioning) seeds the identical baseline (`lib/auth/permissions.ts`'s `DEFAULT_ROLE_GRANTS`, the one canonical list) for every newly created center, so a fresh center's founding Owner is never left with only the four ceiling-forced ownership actions — `check:admin` asserts this directly. `lib/auth/permissions.ts`'s `can()` keeps its exact signature (`can(role, permission, grants)`) but resolves through three tiers: platform-only/PLATFORM_ADMIN/STUDENT settled first, then `OWNER_ONLY_ACTIONS` (`center.ownership.manage`, `permission.manage`, `membership.role.assign`, `membership.branch.grant` — forced true for OWNER with **zero** reliance on any stored row, and hard-denied to every other role regardless of any row), then a small `FORBIDDEN_CEILING` (TEACHER can never hold `center.profit.view`/`analytics.center.view`), and only then the stored grant map — missing means denied. `editableActionsFor(role)` is what the matrix screen and `savePermissionMatrixAction` both call to decide which cells are even writable, so the same ceiling that protects reads also protects writes. `/settings/permissions` is a real action × role grid (`MATRIX_ROLES`: MANAGER/RECEPTIONIST/ACCOUNTANT/TEACHER — OWNER is never a stored row), submitted as one `grant__<role>__<action>` form and written in one transaction (`lib/db/permissions.ts`'s `savePermissionGrants`); the page gate and the action's own `ctxCan(ctx, "permission.manage")` check are independent, so a MANAGER is refused both by UI and by a direct call. Role-granting itself (`createInvitationAction`, `changeMemberRoleAction`) and branch-granting (`grantMembershipBranchAction`/`revokeMembershipBranchAction`, a small "Branch access" card on `/settings/staff`, OWNER-only, listing/adding a MANAGER/RECEPTIONIST/ACCOUNTANT membership's extra branches from real siblings under the same `Organization`) are now gated on `membership.role.assign`/`membership.branch.grant` specifically, not the broader `staff.manage`/`staff.invite` a MANAGER also holds. **Security fix 16**: the teacher-detail page (`/teachers/[id]`) no longer calls `countOtherCenterRelations` at all — the "other branches" `DetailRow` is removed outright, not just hidden, and every remaining query on that page (`db.teachers.findById`, `db.classGroups.list`, `db.studentBlocks.list`) is already `centerId`-scoped, so there is no code path left that could disclose another centre. **Security fix 17**: no code changes were needed beyond documenting the mechanism already in place — `getUserCenterRelations()`/`buildContext()` re-read `Membership`/`PermissionGrant` fresh from the database on every request (React's `cache()` there is per-request only), and no cookie or JWT anywhere in this app carries a role or a grant, so a role/grant change already takes effect on the very next request; `check:permissions` asserts this directly by writing a change and re-reading immediately, with no fresh login and no cache warm-up. Every pre-existing gate that tested the old hardcoded map (`check:analytics`, `check:approvals`, `check:teacher-portal`) is re-pointed at `can()`'s new resolution, and one of them (`check:analytics`) deliberately flips a pre-M12 assertion: a centre **can now** grant RECEPTIONIST `analytics.center.view` via the matrix, since M12 replaces the old narrow per-role allowlist with an open one bounded only by the ceiling above.

## M13 — Global academic catalog + bilingual names + centre-cut rework
**Goal:** subjects, grades and education systems become platform-owned reference data; the FIXED cut gets its per-system default.
**Build:** `CatalogEducationSystem`/`CatalogGrade`/`CatalogSubject` as **global** tables, each with `nameAr` **and** `nameEn`; `CenterCatalogSelection` (a centre picks an entry and edits **only** its own display name and prices); `CatalogSuggestion` + the platform-admin review queue at `/admin/catalog`; `/settings/catalog` replacing the three centre-authored settings screens; migration of existing centre rows into catalog entries + selections. **Centre-cut rework:** `PERCENTAGE` unchanged; **`FIXED` = the exact piastre amount of the session price the centre takes**; each education system carries a **configurable default fixed centre share** (American 40 EGP, IGCSE 50 EGP) used as the default when a class is set to FIXED, **overridable per class and per session**. Person names stay single-valued — no locale variants, ever.
**Done when:** a centre can pick, rename-for-itself and price a catalog entry without touching the canonical row; a suggestion round-trips through the admin queue; every catalog entry renders correctly in both locales; a class set to FIXED inherits its system's default and can override it per class and per session; and **snapshot-at-payment-time is provably unchanged** — changing a system's default moves no existing class and no recorded payment.
**Gates:** `npm run check:catalog` (new — global-vs-scoped boundaries, centre edits confined to name/prices, suggestion queue, bilingual names). *(R8 later removed the suggestion queue and made centre renames bilingual — see R8 below; this entry is the historical record of what M13 shipped.)* **`npm run check:payments` must still pass unchanged** — the cut rework touches money, so the existing snapshot-immutability gate is the one that proves it did not.
**Delivered:** the migration (`prisma/migrations/20260907000000_m13_global_academic_catalog`) backfills one `CatalogEducationSystem`/`CatalogGrade`/`CatalogSubject` row per pre-existing centre-scoped `EducationSystem`/`Grade`/`Subject` row — reusing the old row's own id, the same 1:1-backfill-needs-no-join trick M11 used for `Organization` — plus one `CenterCatalogSelection` row per old row preserving that centre's own name/price as the selection's override; no two pre-existing centre rows are ever merged into one canonical entry. Reusing the id means `ClassGroup.subjectId`/`gradeId`/`educationSystemId` and `StudentProfile.gradeId` needed zero value changes, only a retargeted FK. **Confirmed, not reworked:** `ShareType.FIXED` already meant "the exact piastre amount the centre takes" before this milestone (`lib/money.ts`'s `splitShare`, unchanged) — the brief's "centre-cut rework" framing turned out to be a documentation correction, not a behavior change; `check:payments` passing byte-for-byte unchanged is the proof. **Per-session override, newly added:** `Session.centerShareType`/`centerShareValue` (both nullable, M13) — null on both (every session's state until overridden) means inherit the class's own cut; `lib/db/payments.ts`'s `recordPayment` now reads the session's override first, the class second — `updateSessionShareOverrideAction` (`app/[locale]/(center)/sessions/actions.ts`, `session.manage`-gated, audited) sets or clears it. **The FIXED default inherits at configuration time only, client-side**: `ClassFields` (`components/center/class-fields.tsx`) pre-fills the share-value input from the selected education system's `defaultFixedCenterShare` the moment FIXED is chosen, and stops touching it the instant the person types into the field themselves — there is no server-side "is this still the default" check, because CLAUDE.md rule 2 forbids treating a submitted form value as anything other than what it says. **The display-name override is live on every read, not just `/settings/catalog`:** since nothing in this schema freezes a catalog entry's name the way `priceSnapshot` freezes money, every read is a live join — `lib/db/classes.ts`, `lib/db/sessions.ts`, `lib/db/students.ts`, `lib/db/discover.ts` and the relevant `lib/db/scoped.ts`/`lib/db/student-portal.ts`/`lib/db/teacher-portal.ts` includes all became functions of `centerId` carrying a nested `selections: { where: { centerId } }` lookup, read back by `lib/catalog.ts`'s new `catalogLabelWithSelection`. The one cross-centre single-query read (`listReservationsForStudent`, a student's own reservations across every centre they've applied to) batch-resolves the distinct (centre, catalog id) pairs it actually returns instead, since a nested include can't filter by a sibling row's own `centerId`. Left deliberately canonical-only: ephemeral scheduling-conflict warning text (never a persistent screen) and the platform-admin catalog review queue (deciding the canonical row itself, not viewing through one centre's lens). `npm run check:catalog` proves the propagation directly — a class/session/Discover read at the centre that renamed a subject shows the rename, and the same global subject read at a different centre shows the untouched canonical name. **Discover's subject/grade filter is a real scoped `where`** (`lib/db/discover.ts`'s `discoverClassesForCenter` takes `{ subjectId, gradeId }`), closing the M8 exception the brief called out; the cross-centre per-centre-query-unioned-in-memory shape itself is untouched (that part was never the exception — docs/02-architecture.md §14). `npm run check:payments` passes with zero assertion changes, and `npm run check:catalog` additionally proves the session-override precedence, the config-time-only default (changing a system's `defaultFixedCenterShare` after a class already carries its own value moves that class's value by zero), and the full suggestion round-trip (submit → appears in `/admin/catalog`'s queue → approve creates/corrects the canonical row, or reject creates nothing).

## M14 — Refunds + teacher settlements
**Goal:** complete the money story — money given back, and money paid out.
**Build:** **Refunds** — mark a recorded payment `REFUNDED`, cash back, **no student balance**; the original `Payment` row preserved untouched and a `Refund` record written alongside; analytics **subtracts** the refund from collected revenue **and** from that payment's centre and teacher shares. **Settlements** — `Settlement` (`DRAFT → APPROVED → PAID`, **no partially-paid state**), totals **derived from frozen `teacherShareSnapshot` values and never recomputed**, supporting both a **single session's** teacher share (centres pay cash after each session) and a **period roll-up**; `/settlements` for the centre and `/teacher/settlements` for the teacher. Both permissioned through M12's matrix and audited.
**Done when:** a refund reduces collected revenue and both shares by exactly its amounts, leaves the original row's money fields untouched, and creates no balance anywhere; a settlement's total equals the sum of the frozen snapshots in its scope and **stays correct after the class's cut changes**; and no transition on either is possible without permission or without an audit row.
**Gates:** `npm run check:settlements` (new — refund arithmetic on revenue and both shares, settlement totals derived not recomputed, transition permissions, audit shape). `npm run check:payments` and `npm run check:analytics` must both still pass — this milestone edits the ledger's readers, and those two are what prove it did not edit its history.
**Delivered:** `Refund` and `Settlement` land in one migration (`prisma/migrations/20260907015313_m14_refunds_settlements`), plus `Payment.refunded` (a plain boolean marker, so a reader sees the refunded state without a join). Both are pure readers/derivers of the ledger, never writers of `Payment`'s money fields — `lib/db/refunds.ts`'s `refundPayment` (whole-payment refunds only: `amount`/`centerShareReversed`/`teacherShareReversed` are always exactly the reversed payment's own frozen amounts — no partial-refund shape, since spec §4.1 item 12 only ever asked for "mark a recorded payment REFUNDED") and `lib/db/settlements.ts`'s `createSessionSettlement`/`createPeriodSettlement`/`approveSettlement`/`paySettlement`, both taking `centerId` directly inside a transaction, the same shape `lib/db/payments.ts`'s `recordPayment`/`voidPayment` already use. **Two new matrix actions** (`02-architecture.md` §6): `payment.refund` and `settlement.manage`, both in `MANAGER_BASELINE` (OWNER/MANAGER by default, nothing auto-backfilled onto pre-existing centres per the matrix's own deny-by-default rule) and neither on the ownership/forbidden ceilings — genuinely configurable like any other post-M12 action. **Analytics** (`lib/db/analytics.ts`): `revenueTotals`/`revenueByTeacher` each gained a second aggregate/`groupBy` over `Refund` (denormalizing `Refund.teacherId` from `payment.teacherId`, the same reason `Payment.teacherId` itself is denormalized) and subtract it from the `Payment` aggregate for the same date range/teacher — the refunded payment itself is never voided, so it still contributes to the raw sum; this is what nets it back out. `npm run check:payments` and `npm run check:analytics` pass with **zero assertion changes**, proving the ledger's history is untouched. Duplicate-claiming money is refused outright at creation: `sessionAlreadySettled` (a session already has a settlement, any status) and `overlappingSettlement` (a period settlement overlapping the same teacher's existing scope — overlap is per teacher, so two teachers may share a period). **Screens:** `/refunds` (a read-only ledger; the actual write path is a Refund button on the session roster next to Void — `app/[locale]/(center)/sessions/[id]/session-roster.tsx`), `/settlements` (a period-settlement creation form plus the full register with Approve/Mark Paid actions), a compact "Settle this session's teacher share" action on the session detail page itself for the per-session shape, and `/teacher/settlements` (read-only, unioned across every centre a teacher belongs to, the same `getTeacherContexts` shape the rest of the teacher portal uses).
**M14 follow-up — the refund/settlement interaction, revised.** The first pass let a payment inside a DRAFT/APPROVED settlement's scope be refunded with no effect on that settlement's total, so a centre could still pay a teacher's share on money already given back — flagged and fixed in the same milestone before it shipped further. Decided (`00-master-product-spec.md` §5 "Settlement totals: frozen at creation, live-adjusted while unpaid, immutable once PAID"): `lib/db/refunds.ts`'s `refundPayment` now finds every DRAFT/APPROVED settlement in the refunded payment's scope and subtracts its `teacherShareReversed` from `totalAmount` in the same transaction, audited as a separate `settlement.adjust` row (before/after totals) alongside `payment.refund` — chosen over refusing the refund outright (nothing here can cancel a DRAFT settlement, so a legitimate refund would be stranded) or merely blocking the settlement's next transition without correcting the number (leaves a wrong total visible to the teacher indefinitely). A **PAID** settlement's total is still never touched — the query that finds settlements to adjust excludes PAID entirely, so the original "a settlement already PAID is not rewritten" rule is exactly as strong as before. Also closed: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `00-master-product-spec.md` §2.1.C's CI checklist never had `check:branches`/`check:permissions`/`check:catalog` wired in across M11-M13 — added now, alongside `check:settlements`. Gated by `npm run check:settlements` (`scripts/settlements-check.ts`) — refund arithmetic on revenue and both shares, byte-for-byte Payment immutability after a refund, settlement totals derived-not-recomputed for a config change, a DRAFT settlement's total live-adjusting on a later refund (with the `settlement.adjust`-shaped assertion), a PAID settlement's total staying byte-for-byte unchanged on a later refund, session/period duplicate-claim refusal, every DRAFT→APPROVED→PAID transition (including out-of-order and re-transition refusals), audit shape on every transition, deny-by-default on the two new permissions, and cross-center isolation throughout.

## M15 — Approvals, enrolment, capacity, reschedule + teacher centre-directory onboarding
**Goal:** the rules that govern who gets into a class, and what happens when a session moves.
**Build:** centre approves student enrolment requests at `/approvals`; **teachers get their own approvals page** for students joining their classes; requests check **both `StudentBlock` scopes** and refuse when blocked, at request **and** approval time; a **rejected student cannot re-request the same class for 7 days**, with the UI **saying so** when they try. **Capacity:** enrolment **hard-capped by the more restrictive of the class's own capacity and its room's** (the brief's *"change the room or increase its capacity"* wording assumed the room is always binding, which is wrong when the class's own smaller `capacity` is the actual limiter — see the Delivered note for the corrected, generalized copy); **session attendance may exceed it** (25 in a 20-seat room is fine); a student may still *request* a full class, but staff cannot manually add or approve past the cap. **Reschedule with linkage:** `Session.rescheduledToId` written immediately; target may be a future date or the same day at a different time; the room must be free for the **entire** start→end window; the same rule whether a teacher or centre staff initiates. **Cancelled-session guard** — close spec §7.1's verified gap: server-side refusal of attendance and payment on a `CANCELLED` session, plus a read-only roster. **Teacher onboarding:** a teacher belonging to no centre gets a **centre directory** (`/teacher/centers`), never the student Discover screen, and can request to teach a session or a class pending that centre's approval.
**Done when:** a blocked student is refused at both request and approval; a rejected re-request inside 7 days is refused **and explained**; enrolment past capacity is refused while attendance past capacity succeeds; a reschedule whose target room is free at the start but busy before the end is refused; a cancelled session refuses attendance and payment **by direct action call**, not just in the UI; and a zero-centre teacher lands on the directory.
**Gates:** `npm run check:enrollment` (new — block scopes at both points, the cooling-off, the capacity split, the full-window reschedule, the cancelled-session refusal). `npm run check:classes` and `npm run check:approvals` extended and still green.
**Delivered:** `lib/capacity.ts` is the new single home for two rules that were previously either wrong or unenforced: `enrollmentCap(classCapacity, roomCapacity)` (the smaller of the two non-zero values — a class's own `capacity` field stays a real independent planning target, but the room is the hard ceiling nothing can be configured around) and `isInCooldown`/`cooldownUntil` (the 7-day re-request rule, reading `Reservation.rejectedAt` — a new column, stamped by `rejectReservation`). `lib/db/reservations.ts`'s `reserveOrEnroll`/`confirmReservation` and the centre-staff `enrollStudentAction` (`app/[locale]/(center)/classes/actions.ts`) all call the same capacity helper; the latter also gained the TEACHER-scope block check it was missing (it only checked scope CLASS before this milestone — the same gap the spec's "both scopes, every time" rule exists to close). `/teacher/approvals` (`app/[locale]/(teacher)/teacher/approvals/`) is a real screen reading `scoped(ctx).reservations.listPendingForTeacher(ctx.user.id)` — the exact same `Reservation` rows the centre's own `/approvals` reads, gated by a new matrix action (`reservation.manage.own`, TEACHER baseline) plus a data-scoping check in the Server Action pinning the acted-on reservation to a class this teacher actually teaches. `lib/db/sessions.ts`'s `rescheduleSession` was rewritten to **create a new `Session` row** at the target date/time rather than mutate the original in place, writing the original's `rescheduledToId` in the same transaction — a real behavior change from the pre-M15 version, driven by CLAUDE.md rule 2 (a session that already carries attendance/payments must not have those silently reassigned to a different date by an in-place update) and by the data model's own standing guidance that a reschedule should read as "this session moved to that one," not "this session was always on Thursday." `deriveSessionDisplayStatus` gained a `RESCHEDULED` value, derived from `rescheduledToId` and taking precedence over both the stored `status` and the clock. The cancelled-session guard closing spec §7.1's verified gap (`SessionClosedError` in `lib/db/attendance.ts`, a matching two-line check in `lib/db/payments.ts`'s `recordPayment`) was extended in the same pass to cover a rescheduled session's original slot too, since the same "this session is closed to new activity" logic applies to both terminal states — thrown/returned rather than added to a result-union return type specifically so the ~20 pre-existing test/seed call sites of `createAttendance`/`updateAttendanceStatus` needed zero changes (only the four production Server Actions that must turn it into a user-facing refusal catch it), the same shape `lib/auth/permissions.ts`'s `PermissionError` already established. **Teacher centre-directory onboarding**: `requireTeacherUser()` (`lib/auth/tenant.ts`) mirrors `requireStudentUser()` — it never redirects a zero-relation user away — and `getTenantContext()` now redirects a zero-relation `"teacher"`-experience request to `/teacher/centers` instead of the generic `/select-center` empty state; the `(teacher)` layout branches on `TeacherSession.hasCenter` to render either the ordinary shell or a minimal one around the directory. `/teacher/centers`'s `requestToTeachAction` writes a `NEW_CLASS` `ChangeRequest` directly (no `TenantContext`, since there is no `Membership` yet to resolve one from) resolved through the **exact same** `resolveChangeRequest` path any other `NEW_CLASS` request uses — approving it is a status flip only, never a `Membership` or `ClassGroup` auto-created, identical to the M6-era decision record. The Approvals page (`app/[locale]/(center)/approvals/page.tsx`) gained two small fallbacks this onboarding path needed and the pre-existing connected-teacher flow never did: a requester name resolved from the global `User` when no local `Membership` exists yet, and a subject/grade label resolved from the canonical global catalog entry when the reviewing centre hasn't picked that entry into its own `CenterCatalogSelection`. `/select-center`'s empty state now offers "find a branch to teach at" alongside the existing "browse classes" link. Gated by `npm run check:enrollment` (`scripts/enrollment-check.ts`) — both `StudentBlock` scopes at request and at approval time (a block placed between the two), the cooling-off with an immediate-retry refusal and a post-cooldown success reusing the same row, the capacity split (room-capped enrolment/confirmation vs. uncapped attendance, proven in one run), the full-window reschedule collision under both WARN and DENY, the cancelled- and rescheduled-session guard by direct call to `createAttendance`/`updateAttendanceStatus`/`recordPayment` (never just the UI), and the onboarding write path's use of the ordinary `ChangeRequest`/approvals machinery. `npm run check:classes` gained the reschedule-creates-a-new-row assertions (the original untouched, correctly linked, refusing a second reschedule); `npm run check:approvals` gained the `rejectedAt` stamp and the `reservation.manage.own` permission shape; `npm run check:student-portal`'s pre-existing reject-then-re-request test was updated to account for the new cooldown (immediate retry refused, retry after 7 simulated days succeeds) rather than silently regressing.

## M16 — Parent role & portal
*(Access superseded at F3: phone origination, the privileged-phone refusal and the verify seam are gone; a parent links with a one-time code — see F3 below.)*
**Goal:** a parent can see their children, and nothing more.
**Build:** the **PARENT** role and `ParentLink`; account origination from a phone students listed as their parent phone; a **child selector**; a per-child **mirror of the student portal** (`/parent`), read-only, with **no write path of any kind**. **Refuse** a phone already belonging to a teacher, staff member or platform admin. **A phone match alone never grants access** — the link is inert until an explicit verification/approval step, whose mechanism is **OD-6** and still open: build everything up to it, and leave the step behind the decision rather than inventing one.
**Done when:** a parent sees exactly their verified children and nothing else; a privileged phone is refused; an unverified link grants **zero** reads by UI and by API; and no parent route can write anything.
**Gates:** `npm run check:parent` (new — privileged-phone refusal, inert-until-verified, and the "never a superset of the child" assertion). Isolation matters here — a parent read is a tenant read through a child's `StudentProfile` — so the gate asserts cross-centre denial too.
**Delivered:** `ParentLink` (`parentUserId × studentProfileId`, unique, `centerId` denormalized from the child's own `StudentProfile.centerId`, `status: PENDING | VERIFIED`, `matchedPhone`, `verifiedAt`/`verifiedBy`) plus a new resolved-only `TenantRole` value `PARENT` — `can()` denies it unconditionally, the same shape as `STUDENT`, so no hypothetical stored grant could ever reopen a write path. `lib/db/parents.ts` (no `@/` imports, exercised directly by `npm run check:parent`) is the whole write/read surface: `isPrivilegedPhone` refuses a phone already on an active Membership (checked against **both** the global `User.phone` and a centre-local Membership's own `phone`/`altPhone` — M3's "contact details are centre-local" applies here too) or a platform admin's `User.phone`; `originateParentLinks` checks that refusal **before** matching a student's `parentPhone`/`parentAltPhone` (so a privileged phone that also happens to sit on some student's record — a teacher's own child attending the centre — is refused outright, never silently skipped), then writes PENDING rows only, idempotently (`createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })` against the `(parentUserId, studentProfileId)` unique index); `verifiedChildLinks` — filtered to `status: VERIFIED` — is the **only** read any parent-portal code path is allowed to build on, which is what makes "inert until verified" true by construction rather than by a check some future caller could forget. Wired into account origination at `signUpAction` (`app/[locale]/(auth)/actions.ts`), best-effort (a parent-matching failure must never block an ordinary sign-up). **The OD-6 placeholder seam**: `verifyParentLinkPlaceholder` (`lib/db/parents.ts`) flips one PENDING link to VERIFIED, centre-scoped and one-shot (refuses a second call, refuses the wrong centre's id) — called from a new, clearly-commented `verifyParentLinkAction` (`app/[locale]/(center)/students/actions.ts`), gated by a new matrix action `parent.link.manage` (MANAGER_BASELINE — OWNER/MANAGER by default, configurable like any other post-M12 action) and audited as `parentLink.verify`. Surfaced as a small "Parent accounts" card on `/students/[id]` with a Verify button for PENDING rows — explicitly labeled in code as a temporary stand-in, not OD-6's real answer. **The portal** (`/parent`, `app/[locale]/(parent)/`): `requireParentUser()` (`lib/auth/tenant.ts`) is any signed-in `User` (mirroring `requireStudentUser()`'s "zero relations is a valid state to render" shape) resolving `session.children` through `getVerifiedParentChildren` — VERIFIED links only; a parent with zero of those sees an empty state, never a redirect. The single page is a **read-only mirror of one selected child at a time** (a `?child=` query param, re-validated against `session.children` server-side, never trusted directly) reusing the **exact same** `lib/db/student-portal.ts` functions (`studentUpcomingSessions`/`studentPastAttendance`) the student's own `/student/schedule` page calls against that child's own `centerId`/`studentProfileId` — which is what makes "never a superset of what the child can see" true by construction, not by parallel-implementing a narrower view. The child selector (`ChildSelector`) only ever renders options from that same VERIFIED list. There is **no `actions.ts` anywhere under `app/[locale]/(parent)/`** — `npm run check:parent` asserts this with a static sweep for a `"use server"` directive, not merely that the UI hides a button.

## M17 — Teacher ratings
**Goal:** students rate sessions; the aggregate is useful and the raters stay anonymous.
**Build:** `SessionRating`; a **skippable** login pop-up covering sessions attended in the **last 7 days**, rated 1–5; an aggregate shown on class/session browsing as an **average out of 5 only** — **never the count**; visible to students, centres and the teacher; the teacher can **never** see who rated them; the rating **follows the teacher across centres** (keyed on the global `teacherId`, not a membership).
**Done when:** the prompt is genuinely skippable and never becomes a blocker; the published aggregate exposes an average and nothing else **through the API as well as the UI**; and the same teacher's rating is identical at every centre they work at.
**Gates:** `npm run check:ratings` (new — average-only aggregate, no rater identity and no count on any read path, the 7-day window, cross-centre continuity).
**Delivered:** `SessionRating` (`studentUserId × sessionId` unique, `teacherId` denormalized from `session.teacherId` at rating time — the same reason `Payment.teacherId` is denormalized) and `SessionRatingSkip` (`studentUserId × sessionId` unique, the persisted "never re-prompt" mechanism — deliberately not browser storage, so a skip holds across devices and future logins, not just one tab) land in one migration (`prisma/migrations/20260907154405_m17_session_ratings`). Neither carries a `centerId`: the published aggregate is platform-wide by design, so there is nothing to scope, and neither model has an entry in `lib/db/scoped.ts` — `lib/db/ratings.ts` (no `@/` imports, exercised directly by `npm run check:ratings`) takes `teacherId`/`studentUserId` straight, the same "cross-cutting read, not a `scoped()` entry" shape `lib/db/settlements.ts`/`lib/db/refunds.ts` already use for `centerId`. **The teacher-can-never-see-who-rated-them guarantee is a code-shape property, not just a runtime one**: `lib/db/ratings.ts` exports exactly `attendedSessionsToRate`, `submitSessionRating`, `skipSessionRating`, `teacherRatingAverage` and `teacherRatingAverages` — the last two return/map to `{ average }` alone via `aggregate`/`groupBy`, never a count, and there is no function anywhere in the module (or called by any teacher-facing page) that lists a raw `SessionRating` row by `teacherId`, which is the only way a `studentUserId` could ever reach a teacher; `check:ratings` asserts the exported-function set directly with a static sweep, so a future PR quietly adding a "list ratings" read is caught even though its return shape alone might look safe. `attendedSessionsToRate` (the pop-up's candidate query) is identity-scoped by `studentUserId` across every centre, exactly like `lib/db/student-portal.ts`'s existing cross-centre reads — a session qualifies only with a real `SessionAttendee` row (never merely an `Enrollment`) dated within the last 7 days, with neither a `SessionRating` nor a `SessionRatingSkip` row already against it. **The login pop-up** (`app/[locale]/(student)/rating-popup.tsx`, wired into `app/[locale]/(student)/layout.tsx`) is a client dialog seeded from that query at layout render; closing it (X, backdrop, Escape) persists nothing and never blocks navigation, while each session's own "Skip" button calls `skipSessionRatingAction` (`app/[locale]/(student)/actions.ts`) which writes the persisted skip — the two are deliberately different: a bare dismiss may resurface the same session on a later visit, but an explicit skip never does, on any device. `submitSessionRatingAction`/`submitSessionRating` re-check attendance server-side rather than trusting the pop-up's own list (the same "never trust the client" discipline `reserveOrEnroll` already follows) and refuse a duplicate rating as `alreadyRated`. **Published on three surfaces**, all reading the exact same `{ average }` shape: the student Discover page (`teacherRatingAverages`, batched once across every centre's classes combined — never per centre), the teacher's own dashboard (`teacherRatingAverage(prisma, ctx.user.id)` — no privileged variant exists), and a centre's teacher-detail page (`teacherRatingAverage(prisma, teacher.userId)`). `npm run check:ratings` proves the 7-day window, the attended-vs-enrolled distinction, skip persistence surviving a later independent call (simulating "next login"), the `notAttended`/`alreadyRated` refusals, and that a teacher rated 5 at one throwaway centre and 3 at a second combines into one average of 4 from a single `teacherId`-only call — there is no `centerId` parameter on `teacherRatingAverage` for "which centre's context" to vary in the first place. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gained `check:ratings` — and, closing a gap M16 left open, `check:parent` too, which existed and passed but was never wired into CI.

## M18 — UX & data-integrity sweep + seed rewrite
**Goal:** the accumulated small things that separate a demo from a tool.
**Build:** **visual ID-card layout editor** (drag / resize / add from a palette / remove with ×; a centre never sees JSON). **`lib/phone.ts`** — one normalizer, 11–15 digits, `+20100…`/`20100…`/`0100…` all → `01…`, used by validation, duplicate-matching **and** search. **All search inputs → live type-ahead dropdowns** (debounced, bounded, minimum query length — `02-architecture.md` §14). **One shared date-filter component** — Day / Week (today −7) / Month (1st → today) buttons, specific-date picker, range picker, Apply, reset to today's default, state in the URL. **Soft-delete/deactivate wherever removal could corrupt history or analytics**, hard delete only where nothing references the row, and **fix the 404 on student delete**. **Self-registration creates a full student profile page** (name, grade, email, phone, alt phone, parent details, password). **Receptionist phone lookup reuses M3's duplicate-match panel** — not a raw phone→record endpoint: minimum fields, explicit link action, rate-limited, never another centre's private fields. **Reception scan shows the student's photo** — closing spec §7.1's second verified gap, and the control that justifies the static-QR decision at all. **Seed rewrite:** English person names; grades 1–12 from the global catalog; subjects with Arabic **and** English names; seeded for **Center 60 with 2 branches** so multi-branch behaviour is testable.
**Done when:** the card editor produces a valid layout with no JSON shown; one normalizer is the only place a phone is normalized (asserted, not asserted-by-eye); a reception scan displays the photo; student delete no longer 404s; and `npm run db:seed` produces a two-branch Center 60 that exercises M11's roll-up.
**Gates:** `npm run check:people` extended (the single-normalizer assertion, the lookup's minimum-field/rate-limit shape) and still green; `npm run check:branches` runs against the new seed.
**Delivered — nine separable pieces, each its own commit-sized change:**

1. **Visual ID-card layout editor.** `app/[locale]/(center)/settings/card/card-form.tsx` replaces the raw JSON textarea entirely with direct manipulation on the live card preview: drag to move (pointer events, snapped to a 2% grid, clamped to the face, logical `insetInlineStart`/`top` so it mirrors correctly for Arabic with no extra code), a properties strip to resize (width for the three image elements, font size for the four text elements — text has no `width` field in this data model, so a corner-drag handle would have nothing to write to for most elements; one shared numeric control per element type covers all seven), a palette to add a removed element back, and a `×` to remove one. `lib/id-card.ts`'s `resolveCardLayout` gained the one real behavior change needed to make "remove" mean anything: an element stored as an explicit JSON `null` is now omitted from the resolved layout (and so never rendered), distinct from a merely-absent key (which still means "use the built-in default") — `lib/card-layout.ts` exports the seven known keys (`CARD_ELEMENT_KEYS`) the palette offers. The generated layout is written to a hidden field only at submit; `saveCardLayoutAction`/`cardLayoutSchema` (`app/[locale]/(center)/settings/actions.ts`, `lib/validation/settings.ts`) are unchanged. Below `md`, drag is replaced by a stacked list of elements with numeric x/y/width-or-fontSize inputs, per docs/04 §11.6.
2. **`lib/phone.ts` — the one normalizer.** Moved out of `lib/identity.ts` (which now imports it rather than redefining it) and made the single source every call site imports from: `lib/db/parents.ts`, `lib/db/students.ts` (search), `lib/db/identity.ts` (`createPerson`), and a new `lib/validation/phone-schema.ts` (`optionalPhone`/`requiredPhone`) that collapsed a byte-for-byte duplicate zod schema previously copy-pasted into both `lib/validation/people.ts` and `lib/validation/sessions.ts`. The one real bug this surfaced: `lib/validation/auth.ts`'s registration schema had its own bare regex that validated shape but never normalized, so `signUpAction` could write `User.phone` in a non-canonical form and its ad-hoc `prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { phone } })` duplicate check could miss an existing owner stored differently — now goes through `optionalPhone` like everything else. `npm run check:people` gained a static, database-free gate (`checkSingleNormalizer`) that greps the whole tree for a second `function normalizePhone` or an inline reimplementation, and confirms every `normalizePhone` import resolves to `lib/phone.ts` — an import-graph assertion, not a behavioral one, per the milestone's own instruction that eyeballing isn't enough.
3. **Live type-ahead search.** `components/center/type-ahead-search.tsx` — **300ms debounce, a 2-character minimum query length** (silent below it — no panel at all, per docs/04 §11.8), **results bounded to 8** (matching Reception's pre-existing `take: 8`). Full keyboard support (↑/↓ to move, Enter to select, Escape to close), with the one deliberate exception the design doc calls out: Enter is only intercepted when a suggestion is actually highlighted, so Reception's hardware barcode scanner — which types a code fast and presses Enter — still reaches the plain underlying `<form>` GET submit untouched; the QR/scan path bypasses the dropdown entirely (`parseStudentQrPayload`, unchanged). Wired into every search input found in a full sweep, not just the obvious ones: Students, Teachers, Reception, a teacher's own student roster (`/teacher/students`), a teacher's own session search (`/teacher/sessions`), and three "pick a candidate" pickers — a teacher's block-list (`/teachers/[id]`), a session's add-attendee panel (`/sessions/[id]`), and a class roster's enroll panel (`/classes/[id]`). The three candidate pickers reuse the identical component and bounded/debounced server action shape, but a selected suggestion re-navigates to the *same* screen with `?q=<exact student code>` rather than to a different page — the existing server-rendered candidate list and its permissioned, audited action (block/add/enroll) needed zero changes, since the code-exact query re-runs precisely the lookup that screen already had. `components/center/search-box.tsx` (the plain, non-live predecessor) is deleted — nothing used it anymore. One screen's own date-filter-adjacent search (`/teacher/sessions`) got the same live-search treatment for its "search by student" field, but its **date** filter was deliberately left alone — see item 4.
4. **One shared date-filter component.** `components/shared/date-filter.tsx` + `lib/scheduling.ts`'s `resolveSharedDateFilter`/`lastNDaysUtc`/`monthToDateUtc` + `lib/validation/date-filter.ts`'s `dateFilterSchema` — Day / Week (today −7, a **trailing** window) / Month (1st-of-month → today) quick buttons (submit immediately), a specific-date picker and a custom range picker (both wait for an explicit Apply), and a reset link back to the bare URL (letting each screen's own schema default take over — "day" everywhere now, for consistency). Deliberately **new** primitives, not a repurposing of the pre-existing `weekRangeUtc`/`monthRangeUtc` (real calendar-week/month windows the teacher dashboard's KPIs still use) — "Week"/"Month" mean something different here (a trailing window ending today) than they do there (a calendar window containing an anchor date), and conflating the two would have quietly changed the teacher dashboard's numbers. Replaces the ad-hoc filters on Analytics, Expenses (both previously `components/shared/date-range-filters.tsx`, now deleted) and Sessions (previously its own inline day/week/month select). Analytics/Expenses also dropped "year" and "all-time" — neither was ever in this section's own canonical filter list (Day/Week/Month/specific-date/range), a custom range subsumes "year," and "all-time" has no equivalent here by design (the data-visibility window, M19, is the actual mechanism for "how far back can this role see"). **Not converted, on purpose:** the teacher's own session list (`/teacher/sessions`) keeps its bespoke forward-looking day/week/month filter (anchored-to-today, looking *ahead*) — the opposite of this component's backward-looking definition, and the screen's whole point is "what's coming up" (spec §4.2 item 2), so unifying it in would have inverted its purpose.
5. **Soft-delete/hard-delete audit + the student-delete 404 fix.** An audit of every delete path (students, teachers, classes, rooms, expense categories/expenses, catalog selections, class schedules, holidays, blocks) found the append-only discipline already correctly in place everywhere history could be corrupted (`studentDeleteBlockers`/`teacherDeleteBlockers`/`classDeleteBlockers` refuse a hard delete while attendance/payments/sessions/enrollments reference the row, and every blockable entity already has a deactivate escape hatch) — no gap, no unnecessary new soft-delete ceremony added where none was needed. The actual 404's root cause, traced end to end: the delete control lives on the record's own detail page (`/students/[id]`), the action hard-deletes and never redirects, and Next's automatic post-Server-Action refresh of the *current* route re-fetches the now-gone row and renders `notFound()` — the list page's own `revalidatePath` was correct but irrelevant, since the user was never looking at the list. The same shape existed in two more places once looked for: `deleteTeacherAction` and `deleteClassAction`. Fixed identically in all three — `redirect()` (`lib/i18n/navigation.ts`) to the list route on success — closing the class of bug, not just the one reported instance.
6. **Self-registration full profile.** See spec §4.3 item 4 for the full delivery note. Short version: a new nullable `StudentProfile.profileCompletedAt` distinguishes an auto-seeded stub (`ensureStudentProfile`, M8 — never set) from a whole record (receptionist-created, set at creation); the `(student)` layout substitutes a one-time completion form (`ProfileCompletionForm`, reusing the receptionist add-student form's exact field components and zod schema) for whatever page was requested, for every route in the group, until that column is stamped — no redirect, no pathname check, and no way to reach the rest of the portal with an incomplete profile.
